# K4 negatives: what is known NOT to work

Generated by `python -m kryptos negatives` from `negatives/community.jsonl` (sourced, hand-curated) and `experiments/ledger.csv` (our own runs). Don't edit this file; edit those and rebuild.

Read this before trying a new idea. If your idea is below, either skip it or say exactly how your version differs (wider scope, different alphabet, stacked with something else).

**406 community entries** (209 partial or weak test, 136 solid elimination, 38 sanborn / scheidt statement, 14 rejected claimed solution, 9 disputed) and **355 of our own null runs** (4,894,977,973,172,552 hypotheses).

## statistics

- **Pure transposition, or monoalphabetic substitution followed by transposition**
  - Scope: IoC argument on the 97 carved letters
  - Outcome: Ruled out: K4 IoC 0.036 vs about 0.066 for English, so a non-transposition substitution layer is present
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **K4's 97 letters fill a complete rectangular grid with no padding or nulls**
  - Scope: Length of the carved K4 ciphertext
  - Outcome: Ruled out: 97 is prime, so any grid needs padding, ragged rows or a null
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **A candidate decryption may put the known plaintext at other positions**
  - Scope: Sanborn's released cribs: EASTNORTHEAST at 22-34 and BERLINCLOCK at 64-74 (24 letters, 1-indexed)
  - Outcome: Ruled out: every candidate must place these 24 letters exactly at these positions
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler; cribs released by Jim Sanborn 2010-2020 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **Pure transposition, alone or in any combination of transpositions**
  - Scope: Letter-count proof: the cribs need 3 E's but the 97-letter ciphertext has only 2
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof (stronger than the IoC argument): some layer must change letters
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **Ciphers that never encrypt a letter to itself (Enigma with reflector, Porta, Playfair)**
  - Scope: Two crib fixed points: K->K at 74 and also S->S at 33 (existing entries cite only K->K)
  - Outcome: Ruled out: two independent self-encryptions confirm fixed points are allowed
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **Fits at key length 19 (or 38) are evidence for that period**
  - Scope: Crib pair R->P occurs twice, at 28 and 66 (38 apart); the only repeated plain/cipher pair
  - Outcome: Observation: this one repeat explains many 'lucky' fits at length 19; treat such fits as expected, not signal
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Direct periodic substitution with period 7, 14, 21 or 42 (any scheme reusing one substitution 42 letters apart)**
  - Scope: Crib clashes 42 apart: T->V at 25 vs L->V at 67, and A->K at 32 vs K->K at 74
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof for any alphabet; wider than the Gromark-only period-21/42 result
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **One fixed substitution alphabet per carved row**
  - Scope: Adjacent crib letters N->Q at 26 and O->Q at 27 on the same row
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof: two plaintext letters map to Q within one row
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Doubled ciphertext letters are placed at random**
  - Scope: Doublets at 19 (BB), 26 (QQ), 33 (SS), 43 (SS), 47 (ZZ), 68 (TT)
  - Outcome: Observation: 5 of the 6 doublets share the same position mod 7; significance discussed in the 7/21-rhythm entry
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Observation: K4 has five W's at regular-looking spacing**
  - Scope: W at positions 21, 37, 49, 59, 75
  - Outcome: Fact underlying the W-separator theory; simple separator versions all fail (see w-delimiter entries)
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Observation: Y and M each appear only once in K4**
  - Scope: Letter counts of the 97-letter ciphertext. TRIED.md attributes the note to Jim Gillogly. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Fact; no method derived from it
  - Solid elimination · Jim Gillogly (early observation); TRIED.md compiler (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **A short repeating key alone (detectable by Kasiski or Friedman tests)**
  - Scope: Kasiski and Friedman tests; entropy 4.55 bits/letter (random ceiling) vs 4.35-4.39 for K1/K2
  - Outcome: Ruled out: no period shows; if a short memorable key was used it was expanded nonlinearly
  - Solid elimination · TRIED.md compiler (checked with its own code) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **K4 involves transposition (tested statistically against letter-difference and width-21/width-7 regularities)**
  - Scope: Permutation tests: width-21 vertical bigram repeats (11 of 76, ~1 in 6,750); Materna minor differences (~1 in 5,520); repeated-plaintext cipher distances (p ~1/240-1/1,470)
  - Outcome: Bean argues one-to-one single-letter encryption, i.e. no transposition; tests were chosen after looking at the data, so suggestive not conclusive
  - Partial or weak test · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **K4 is a single ACA cipher type**
  - Scope: Argument from low IoC (~0.036) and the 2020 clues
  - Outcome: Commenter argues a combination of two or more types is needed; opinion with partial evidence, no test
  - Partial or weak test · elahieh (Hacker News), 2025-08-15 · [source](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=44907366)
- **The Ws in K4 are word or segment separators (W-delimiter hypothesis)**
  - Scope: Every simple divider version, plus a 2026 audit deleting non-W letters
  - Outcome: All divider versions failed; audit found the oddity also appears when non-W letters are deleted, so it says nothing about the Ws
  - Partial or weak test · kryptosbot.com (Colin Patrick with Claude), 2026-10-12 · [source](https://www.kryptosbot.com/)
- **Stehle's DIAWINFBN run (five equal letter differences at lag 4) is a chance artifact**
  - Scope: 200,000 shuffled K4s; statistic = longest run of constant difference over any lag 1-20 (corrects for lag choice)
  - Outcome: Not chance by this test: p=0.0032. Caveat: found post hoc among many patterns, so the true p is weaker. Points at lag-4 structure.
  - Partial or weak test · this repo (campaign idea 16), 2026-11-15 · campaign/results/16.json
- **K4's doubled letters (5 of 6 on one residue mod 7) are a chance artifact**
  - Scope: 200,000 shuffled K4s; statistic = best excess concentration over moduli 2-12 (corrects for modulus choice)
  - Outcome: Not chance by this test: p=0.0011 overall, 0.0072 among shuffles with >=6 doublets. Caveat: post hoc. Supports a period-7 / width-7 component.
  - Partial or weak test · this repo (campaign idea 16), 2026-11-15 · campaign/results/16.json
- **K4 looks like the output of a standard cipher family (29 simulated families)**
  - Scope: 4,000 simulated K4-like English messages per family; statistics: IoC, Stehle run, doublet clustering, width-w repeated pairs, close letters
  - Outcome: No family matched all five K4 statistics at once (p<0.00025 each). Most K4-like: substitution with no transposition and short/structured keys; least: running keys, OTP, transposition stacks. Weak evidence (calibration median ranks 7-23 of 29).
  - Partial or weak test · this repo (campaign idea 18), 2026-11-15 · campaign/FINGERPRINTS.md
- **Observation: the 7/21 rhythm (width-21 vertical repeats, width-7 doublets)**
  - Scope: 11 repeated vertical pairs at width 21; 5 of 6 doublets align mod 7; inside the cribs every width-7 column 5->6 step is a doublet; look-elsewhere correction
  - Outcome: ~1 in 4,000 raw, ~1 in 90-210 after best-width correction, ~1 in 10 without the doublets; a hint, not a method
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4, reggiechan74/kryptos-k4 (building on Bean) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Observation: plaintext letters in KRYPTOS encrypt to within +/-2 of themselves**
  - Scope: 9 of 10 known cases; barneyjm re-test (https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4); first noted by Greg (r/UnresolvedMysteries 2020)
  - Outcome: Post hoc: Barney gets 1 in 4,500 for the subset, 0.14 for all letters; do not use as a hard filter
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4, barneyjm/kryptos-k4, Greg (Reddit) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Observation: the implied key looks random (high linear complexity)**
  - Scope: Linear complexity of crib-implied keys
  - Outcome: If a short key was used, it was expanded nonlinearly
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)

## sanborn-statement

- **Kryptos had a prominent fiction-writer co-author**
  - Outcome: He considered it but decided not to (secondhand via FAQ)
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via FAQ) · [source](https://elonka.com/kryptos/faq.html)
- **The lower half of Kryptos uses a single encipherment system**
  - Scope: 1990 dedication remarks. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn: the lower half uses two separate systems, 'and that is a major clue in itself'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), 1990 · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **K4 reuses the K1-K3 methodology**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'There was a change in the methodology'; passages made harder as the text progressed
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2000-03-17 · [source](https://www.baltimoresun.com/2000/03/17/unlocking-the-secret-of-kryptos-cryptogram-for-nearly-a-decade-a-jumble-of-seemingly-random-letters-on-a-sculpture-at-cia-headquarters-has-mystified-experts-who-have-tried-to-decipher-its-code/)
- **K4 is unsolvable / a true one-time pad**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'It ain't easy, but it's solvable!'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2003 · [source](https://elonka.com/kryptos/faq.html)
- **K4 is a textbook system that Scheidt alone could decipher**
  - Outcome: Sanborn counts five or six techniques; he modified Scheidt's systems, used matrix codes, and designed individualistic 'visual systems for encoding'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **K4 can be solved independently of K1-K3**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'I have left instructions in the earlier text that refer to later text'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **A correct K4 decryption must be letter-perfect English**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'most of my things are rife with mistakes on purpose'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **K4's plaintext is the end of the puzzle**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: decipher, then go to the agency and find that place; riddle follows (K5)
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **Being on CIA grounds is needed to decrypt K4**
  - Outcome: No; physical location matters only for the riddle
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **K4 is exactly 97 characters with no ambiguity**
  - Outcome: Sanborn calls it 97; the '?' may belong to K4; Scheidt said 'I think there are 98 characters left'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn / Ed Scheidt, 2005 · [source](https://elonka.com/kryptos/faq.html)
- **Webster's sealed 1990 envelope holds the K4 solution**
  - Outcome: False: 'I definitely didn't give him the last section'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **K4 relates to covert obsolescence like the Cyrillic Projector**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'No'; a very different animal
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2005 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/business/1,66333-0.html)
- **A K4 solution could not be confirmed if Sanborn died**
  - Scope: 2005 remark. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn said he set up a way to confirm a solution if he died first
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), 2005 · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **K4 uses the same process as K1-K3**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: four processes, two similar and two different; K4's English was deliberately masked
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2005-01-21 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/culture/0,1284,66359,00.html)
- **K4's masking step is a known, published technique**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'The masking technique may not be known'; kept secret; solve technique first
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2005-01-21 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/culture/0,1284,66359,00.html)
- **K4 plaintext is in a language other than English**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'All four (sections) are done in the English language'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2005-01-21 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/culture/0,1284,66359,00.html)
- **A readable K4 plaintext is the final message in plain natural English**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: a readable message may have other encoding (code words like 'cake')
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2005-01-21 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/culture/0,1284,66359,00.html)
- **Scheidt's design recollection exactly matches what was carved**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: Sanborn had the chisel, so there could be some changes
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt, 2005-01-21 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/culture/0,1284,66334,00.html)
- **Letter-frequency analysis works on K4 as on K1-K3**
  - Outcome: Scheidt said frequency counting helps on parts 1-3 but he deliberately masked that in part 4
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt (NPR), 2005-06-09 · [source](https://elonka.com/kryptos/mirrors/npr/2005transcript.html)
- **K1-K3 misspellings (IQLUSION etc.) are accidental**
  - Outcome: Sanborn admitted introducing misspellings to add difficulty; IQLUSION intentional (NYT 2020)
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2010-11-21 · [source](http://www.nytimes.com/2010/11/21/us/21code.html)
- **K4 requires a complex mathematical procedure**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'not mathematical... simple, can be remembered'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt (quoted by Bean), 2011 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **K4 is a single-stage cipher**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'more than one stage'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt (quoted by Bean), 2015 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **BERLINCLOCK plaintext is transposed away from its ciphertext positions**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: BERLINCLOCK matches directly with NYPVTTMZFPK, a one-to-one match
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (Bogart 2019, quoted by Bean), 2019 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Only the intended method counts as a K4 solution**
  - Scope: 2019 remark. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn said he 'did not care' what method a solver uses
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), 2019 · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **K4 is encrypted directly on the English letter base**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: 'change the language base'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt (quoted by Bean), 2020 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Plaintext positions 22-25 are unconstrained (only NORTHEAST 26-34 was released)**
  - Scope: Clue given to the Kryptos group, Apr-Aug 2020
  - Outcome: Sanborn released EAST at 22-25, completing EASTNORTHEAST at 22-34
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), 2020 · [source](https://scienceblogs.de/klausis-krypto-kolumne/2020/08/24/jim-sanborn-publishes-another-new-kryptos-clue/)
- **K4 is a single-layer cipher without extra obfuscation**
  - Outcome: Scheidt: last passage uses a masking technique, a further level of obfuscation; 97-length itself a challenge
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Ed Scheidt (email to NYT), 2020-01-29 · [source](https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2020/01/29/climate/kryptos-sculpture-final-clue.html)
- **K4's plaintext includes nulls/padding, or the cribs are not at their stated positions**
  - Scope: Kobek and Byrne's description of the plaintext strips found in Sanborn's Smithsonian papers
  - Outcome: Plaintext is exactly 97 letters, makes semantic sense, clues in the right places; method unknown, files sealed to 2075
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jarett Kobek and Richard Byrne (via TRIED.md compilation), 2025-09 · [source](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/a-solution-to-the-cias-kryptos-code-is-found-after-35-years/)
- **Recovering the K4 plaintext reveals the method**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'They did not solve K4 and they certainly did not find the key'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2025-10-23 · [source](https://content.rrauction.com/kryptos-k4-discovered-not-solved-heres-what-actually-happened/)
- **K5 is unrelated to the K2 text**
  - Scope: Nov 2025 final clues (clue 4)
  - Outcome: Sanborn: K5 links to the K2 line 'it's buried out there somewhere'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), 2025-11 · [source](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/cia-kryptos-puzzle-creator-releases-final-clues/)
- **K4 has a purely mathematical solution**
  - Outcome: Sanborn: 'Who says it is even a math solution?'; urges creativity
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2025-11-12 · [source](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/cia-kryptos-puzzle-creator-releases-final-clues/)
- **K4 key depends on prior plaintext/ciphertext (e.g. autokey) rather than position**
  - Outcome: K5 is 97 characters and shares some coded words in the same positions as K4, suggesting a position-dependent key (inference)
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2025-11-12 · [source](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/cia-kryptos-puzzle-creator-releases-final-clues/)
- **BERLINCLOCK is unrelated to the Weltzeituhr; plaintext themes unconstrained**
  - Outcome: Plaintext involves 1986 Egypt trip and 1989 Berlin Wall fall; codes concern delivering a message; BERLINCLOCK = World Clock, Alexanderplatz
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn, 2025-11-12 · [source](https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/cia-kryptos-puzzle-creator-releases-final-clues/)
- **K4 was produced by a computer-only process or by the K1-K3 charts**
  - Outcome: Lot lists a separate 'original coding system for K4', K1-K3 coding charts, and handwritten plaintexts for screen cutting, pointing to a paper process
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · RR Auction lot 2001 (Sanborn archive), 2025-11-20 · [source](https://www.rrauction.com/auctions/lot-detail/350761607302001-the-complete-secrets-of-kryptos-jim-sanborns-private-archive/)
- **'Masking' is a step Sanborn himself designed and can explain**
  - Scope: Interviews (various). Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn was confused by questions about masking; it is Scheidt's term, not his
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), various · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **Solving K4 requires a particular book (e.g. a book-derived running key)**
  - Scope: Interviews (various). Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn: you don't need a particular book, 'just to be able to read'
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), various · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **K1-K3 must be solved before K4 can be solved**
  - Scope: Interviews (various); compare 2006 remark that K1-K3 answers contain clues to K4. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Sanborn: you don't need K1-K3 solved to solve K4 (though their answers hold clues)
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn (via TRIED.md compilation), various · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)
- **K4 contains a deliberate carving error like the K2 slip**
  - Scope: Statements by both makers. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Both say K4 is solvable and the K2 slip was the only one (contrast: 'rife with mistakes on purpose')
  - Sanborn / Scheidt statement · Jim Sanborn and Ed Scheidt (via TRIED.md compilation), various · [source](https://www.kryptools.com.about.ag/hints.htm)

## periodic-substitution

- **Periodic Vigenere, Beaufort or Variant Beaufort**
  - Scope: Periods 1-48; A-Z and KRYPTOS alphabets; both cribs (22-34, 64-74)
  - Outcome: Ruled out; periods 27-29 fit only because the cribs place no constraint there; planted-solution tests confirm detection
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **Porta, Playfair, or any cipher that cannot map a letter to itself**
  - Scope: Self-encryption K->K at position 74
  - Outcome: Ruled out by the K->K fixed point at position 74
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Repeating-key tableau cipher (Vigenere/Beaufort/Variant over 8 alphabet families)**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26, all 8 families; exact crib-constraint proof
  - Outcome: Ruled out at 1-26; lengths >=27 not constrained by the cribs; validated on 21 planted ciphers
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Progressive key K[i mod p] + s*floor(i/p)**
  - Scope: p <= 26, all 8 tableau families
  - Outcome: Ruled out; only one-constraint survivors at p=26, as chance predicts
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Digit keys: Gronsfeld, Gromark, or any 0-9 keystream**
  - Scope: A-Z, KRYPTOS and all 209k dictionary alphabets
  - Outcome: Ruled out: no alphabet makes all 24 shifts digits (best 21/24); does not cover arbitrary-alphabet Gromark
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Key arithmetic mod 27-31 instead of 26 (the ? as a 27th symbol; 30-31 wide tableau rows)**
  - Scope: 220,318 keyword alphabets x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort x N=27..31; repeating, sliding, progressive, autokey, stacked (necessary test), chain; multiplying key mod 29/31; 11.5M key streams
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 pass with >=7 checks (3 shuffled K4s: 0); planted mod-27 and mod-31 sliding keys found 3/3
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign 11), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_11.md
- **Multiplier word + adder word: C = A[i mod p]*P + B[i mod q] mod 26**
  - Scope: A-Z/KRYPTOS each side, p,q=1..17, exact linear solve (1,156 systems); 220,318 keyword alphabets x 5 layouts x 10 fixed multipliers x battery of key rules (11.0M key streams)
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 fits (3 shuffled K4s: 0); planted 5/5
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign 11), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_11.md
- **Monoalphabetic substitution alone**
  - Scope: Crib proof: ciphertext F stands for E at 22 and for O at 72
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof
  - Solid elimination · everyone (standard crib argument) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **Key restarts at the start of both cribs**
  - Scope: Any key length, any alphabet; also shown by markmnl (https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof: 3 letters in, T->V and L->V clash
  - Solid elimination · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4, markmnl/kryptos (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Simple monoalphabetic substitution (with Vigenere and transposition also judged unlikely)**
  - Scope: Informal reasoning from the cribs and doubled letters
  - Outcome: Mono ruled out: plaintext I and N both map to TT; transposition unlikely; keyword Vigenere apparently not; 6 doubled pairs not significant
  - Partial or weak test · Nick Pelling (Cipher Mysteries), 2011-11-07 · [source](https://ciphermysteries.com/2011/11/07/kryptos-sculpture-update)
- **Chained ciphers with Vigenere keyed by an English dictionary word**
  - Scope: Every 8-letter English dictionary word; Python then Rust (~3h48m); other chained cipher types not named
  - Outcome: No matches
  - Partial or weak test · 'Asynchronous' blog (code written with Gemini 2.5 Pro), 2025-04-03 · [source](https://asynchronous-x-github-io.pages.dev/post/attempting-kryptos-k4)
- **Periodic Vigenere/Beaufort including keyword-alphabet (Quagmire) and hand-modified alphabets**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26; standard and KRYPTOS alphabets; exact and up to 2 crib errors; ~13k keyword-alphabet pairs; 1,850 one-move/two-swap alphabets
  - Outcome: All dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Progressive and polynomial key schedules**
  - Scope: Mod 26, 24, 12 and 60
  - Outcome: All dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Key generated by a linear rule mod 26**
  - Scope: 1,544 rules with up to 5 terms
  - Outcome: Dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Separate periodic keys for each half of K4 ('Divided Berlin')**
  - Scope: 13,456 key-length pairs
  - Outcome: No fit
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Gronsfeld, Gromark or clock-digit keys with dictionary alphabets**
  - Scope: Dictionary-derived alphabets
  - Outcome: No alphabet keeps all 24 implied key values below 16
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Recurrence-distance key clock**
  - Scope: Study 064
  - Outcome: 6 periods excluded plus period 5 newly incompatible
  - Partial or weak test · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4, 2026-10/11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Repeating or progressive key allowing 1-2 wrong crib letters (carving errors)**
  - Scope: Repeating and progressive keys x 1.8M alphabets (wider than the standard/KRYPTOS + 13k-pair crib-error run already recorded)
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Progressive key (key shifts each cycle)**
  - Scope: Periods up to 26
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **PALIMPSEST or ABSCISSA (the K1/K2 keys) used directly as the K4 key**
  - Scope: Every position/offset. A special case of keithadler's length 1-26 repeating-key proof. Source is the closest one listed in TRIED.md; no direct URL given for this item.
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · many (unnamed community testers) (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/keithadler/kryptos)
- **Affine or mixed Vigenere/Beaufort rule per key position**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-10
  - Outcome: Ruled out for lengths 1-10; undecidable beyond
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Dictionary-word Vigenere keys applied to cyclic shifts of K4**
  - Scope: Word-list keys, KRYPTOS alphabet. No direct URL in TRIED.md for this tester; cited via the reggiechan74 external prior-art register that TRIED.md drew on.
  - Outcome: No signal
  - Partial or weak test · mfascia (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Keyword written through a grid, block-constant keys, keyword plus per-letter drift**
  - Scope: 3,366 models x 1.8M alphabets
  - Outcome: No signal: 148 fits vs 197 expected by chance, all gibberish
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Interrupted keys (restart/advance/pause on triggers, word breaks, carved lines)**
  - Scope: 11,077 models x 842k alphabets
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)

## keyed-alphabet

- **KRYPTOS-keyed alphabet Vigenere with a dictionary secondary key (also applied to Karl Wang's 21/49-column transposition outputs)**
  - Scope: 233,383 NLTK English words of 4-17 letters; BERLINCLOCK-contained oracle
  - Outcome: No hit; author says it rules out only Vigenere alone in that configuration
  - Solid elimination · Mireya Jurado (Spray on Security), 2021-08-01 · [source](https://sprayonsecurity.com/kryptos-k4-is-not-a-vigenere-cipher/)
- **Periodic substitution with arbitrary alphabets (every Quagmire, every keyword)**
  - Scope: Periods 1-48
  - Outcome: Impossible at periods 1-7, 9, 10, 14, 15, 17, 21, 25, 34, 42, 43, 45; all other periods up to 48 unconstrained by the cribs
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **Tableau ciphers with theme-word keyed alphabets (PALIMPSEST, ABSCISSA, WELTZEITUHR...)**
  - Scope: 20 theme-word alphabets x 8 families
  - Outcome: Ruled out; best survivors hold 3 constraints vs ~30 expected by chance
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Vigenere/Beaufort with dictionary-word keyed alphabets**
  - Scope: 209k words x 5 arrangements
  - Outcome: Ruled out; all 68k survivors sit at p=26 on one constraint
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **General Quagmire I/II with an arbitrary alphabet**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26
  - Outcome: Ruled out at 1-12, 14, 15, 17, 18, 21, 22, 25 (some variants also 13, 20, 23); other lengths hold <=1 constraint, untestable
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **General Quagmire III with an arbitrary alphabet (Vigenere and Beaufort forms)**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26; 30-minute solver timeout
  - Outcome: Vigenere form ruled out at all 1-26; Beaufort form ruled out at 1-12, 14-17, 19-22, 24, 25; 13 and 18 unresolved; 23, 26 satisfiable but so are random texts
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Arbitrary independent alphabets per key class (periodic)**
  - Scope: Periods 1-60; standard, KA and Greek alphabets for 27-60
  - Outcome: Inconsistent at 1-7, 9, 10, 14, 15, 17, 21, 25; EASTNORTHEAST alone excludes 1-5, 9; periods 19 and 38 undecidable; 27-60 inconsistent
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **General Quagmire IV (two independent arbitrary alphabets)**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26
  - Outcome: Ruled out at 1-7, 9, 10, 15, 17, 22, 25; satisfiable at 8, 13, 16, 19, 20, 23, 24, 26 but random texts pass at similar rates
  - Partial or weak test · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Quagmire I/II at crib-untestable key lengths, found by English-scored annealing**
  - Scope: Quagmire I L=13, 16, 20, 24; Quagmire II L=13, 16; quadgram scoring validated on planted ciphers
  - Outcome: K4 scores no better than random ciphertexts (-4.6 to -5.7 vs -4.8 to -5.5 per quadgram); uninformative for two free alphabets at L=8, QII L>=20, QI L=26
  - Partial or weak test · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Arbitrary monoalphabetic mask plus periodic key**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26
  - Outcome: Inconsistent for 1-12, 14, 15, 17, 21, 22; lengths 13, 16, 19, 20, 23, 24, 26 underdetermined
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Keyed alphabets from phrases in Sanborn's own words**
  - Scope: 613,440 alphabets from his oral history, K1-K3, Morse and clue quotes
  - Outcome: Dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Independent substitution per position class**
  - Scope: Period lengths up to 25 (session 2)
  - Outcome: Impossible for lengths 1-7, 9, 10, 14, 15, 17, 21 and 25
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10-17 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Vigenere/Beaufort/Variant with keyword-mixed alphabets**
  - Scope: Dictionary and phrase keyword alphabets (TRIED.md range ~209k-842k words, German words, oral-history phrases)
  - Outcome: Catalog negative: every listed alphabet failed
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Periodic Vigenere/Beaufort with dictionary keyword-mixed alphabets**
  - Scope: Up to 842k dictionary keyword alphabets plus German words (wider than the ~13k pairs in periodic-vig-beau-quagmire-barneyjm)
  - Outcome: Catalog negative
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **General Quagmire IV (two independent arbitrary alphabets)**
  - Scope: Key lengths 1-26
  - Outcome: Ruled out at 1-7, 9, 10, 15, 17, 22, 25; other lengths fit no more often than random text
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Quagmire cases found by English-scored simulated annealing**
  - Scope: Settings that recovered planted ciphers at 88-97 of 97 letters
  - Outcome: No signal: K4 scores like random text
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Arbitrary mask alphabet plus grid, progressive, two-key or keyword-through-grid keys**
  - Scope: 14,408 models
  - Outcome: No signal: survivors match shuffled-text rates
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Keyword-mixed 'masking chart' plus a small aperiodic key**
  - Scope: Various charts
  - Outcome: Catalog negative
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Real words adjacent to the cribs, re-testing open mask periods**
  - Scope: 3,497 words
  - Outcome: No signal: real words fit less often than random strings
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Ragbaby cipher**
  - Scope: Shared alphabet; also Scott (https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos, scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Quagmire III keyed with the first 11 characters of K1**
  - Scope: Single key, second-hand report
  - Outcome: Reportedly gave BERLIN at 64-69 but did not extend; general case now ruled out by keithadler's exact Quagmire III search
  - Disputed · 'Scott' (reported by Christian Schridde), 2017 · [source](https://www.numberworld.blog/2017/03/kryptos-cipher-part-2.html)

## autokey

- **Plaintext and ciphertext autokey**
  - Scope: Primer lengths 1-96; A-Z and KRYPTOS alphabets
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **Ciphertext- or plaintext-keyed autokey**
  - Scope: Every lag, 8 tableau families
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Autokey (plaintext or ciphertext source), Condi and Ragbaby with a hidden alphabet**
  - Scope: x[C]=a*x[P]+b*x[U]+c mod 26 solved exactly for the alphabet; a,b=+-1; alphabet hidden on both sides (decided at lag 1 only), or hidden on one side with A-Z/KRYPTOS on the other (decided at lags 1-6); Ragbaby word-progressive in 5 alphabet modes; 330 models
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: 271 models contradicted, 0 consistent with >=3 checks (20 shuffled K4s: 0 each); 59 undecidable (hidden both sides at lag >=3; one side at lag 7-8). Planted lag-1 autokeys found 6/6
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign idea 55), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_9.md
- **Autokey with a keyed distance: key letter = letter L[i mod p] places back, plaintext or ciphertext feed**
  - Scope: 220,318 keyword alphabets (M/M) + A-Z/KRYPTOS views; Vigenere/Beaufort; cipher feed lags 1-20, plain feed lags 1-8; periods 1-13; 596M settings, exact
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 fits (3 shuffled K4s: 0); planted 4/4
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign 11), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_11.md
- **Plaintext autokey, motivated by interval-7 roughness in K4**
  - Scope: Unspecified number of attempts
  - Outcome: No solution at the time; scope unknown, so weak as an elimination
  - Partial or weak test · NSA cryptanalysts, 1992 · [source](https://documents.theblackvault.com/documents/controversies/KRYPTOS-Statue-NSA.pdf)
- **Autokey (also with dictionary alphabets)**
  - Scope: Lags 1-96 with dictionary alphabets
  - Outcome: Dead; best 10/24, equal to the null
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Short-primer subtractive feedback key**
  - Scope: Bounded study 059; exact bounds in linked receipt files
  - Outcome: Bounded exclusion; the subtractive feedback itself is untested
  - Partial or weak test · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4, 2026-10/11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Plaintext and ciphertext autokey**
  - Scope: Exact algebra, plain/KRYPTOS alphabets: PT lags 1-26 and 30-52, CT lags 1-73; dictionary alphabets; near-miss search (707M configs)
  - Outcome: Ruled out in those ranges; near misses top out at 10/24, same as shuffled text
  - Partial or weak test · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Backward autokey (key taken from later letters)**
  - Scope: Lags 1-96
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Autokey with an arbitrary 'mask' alphabet**
  - Scope: All lags
  - Outcome: Ruled out

## running-key

- **Running key from the K1-K3 plaintext**

- **Running key from Carter's Tomb of Tut-ankh-Amen**
  - Scope: Vols 1-2; A-Z and KRYPTOS alphabets; every offset, forwards and reversed
  - Outcome: Ruled out; best 8 of 24
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **Running key from K1-K3 plaintexts, the carved left side or the tableau, incl. arbitrary alphabet on one side**
  - Scope: Forward and reversed, every offset, 8 families; plus 26! alphabet on one side with carved, corrected and pre-2006 spellings (incl. IDBYROWS) and K1-K3 ciphertext: 141k tests
  - Outcome: Ruled out; 0 survivors in the arbitrary-alphabet tests

## transposition

- **K3-style grid rotation (one rotation, or two at any pair of widths) with tableau key**
  - Scope: 30M combinations
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Transposition applied to the repeating key instead of the text: C_i = P_i + key[perm(i) mod p]**
  - Scope: perm (and inverse): every columnar order widths 2-8, dictionary keyword orders widths 9-14, single and double K3-style rotations at widths 2-48, columns/snake/diagonal/spiral routes widths 2-48, skips a*i+b mod 97, grid-linear slots alpha*col+beta*row mod p; p=2-26; Vigenere/Beaufort; A-Z/KRYPTOS each side; 73,010,208 settings, exact
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 settings with >=6 checks (5 shuffled K4s: 0); planted double-rotation and columnar key scrambles found 4/4 with 17-19 checks
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign idea 61), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_9.md
- **Geographic or keyword column orders**
  - Scope: Widths 15-26, 353,100 orders
  - Outcome: Rejected; known only from a search snippet, page not fetched

- **K3-style double rotation (with periodic keys <=12)**
  - Scope: Widths 2-96, 72,200 permutations
  - Outcome: 0 hits
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **10x10 turning grille**

## polygraphic

- **Hill 3x3 with A=0..Z=25, block at BERLINCLOCK position 64**
  - Scope: Single block alignment
  - Outcome: Eliminated: Det(S)*20 = 23 (mod 26) has no solution
  - Solid elimination · Christian Schridde (NumberWorld), 2017 · [source](https://www.numberworld.blog/2017/03/kryptos-cipher-part-2.html)
- **Hill 3x3 with A=1..Z=26**
  - Scope: Solvable mod 13; lifted to mod 26 over all 2^9 choices
  - Outcome: Eliminated: no valid mod-26 solution
  - Solid elimination · Christian Schridde (NumberWorld), 2017 · [source](https://www.numberworld.blog/2017/03/kryptos-cipher-part-2.html)
- **Seriated digraphic cipher (seriated Playfair, Doppelkasten) at width 21**
  - Scope: Crib constraints on vertical bigrams
  - Outcome: Ruled out: plaintext bigrams ending in I and N would both map to ZT; K->K kills standard Playfair on vertical bigrams
  - Solid elimination · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Seriated digraphic cipher at width 7**
  - Scope: Crib constraints
  - Outcome: Ruled out: plaintext bigrams ending in N and O would both map to BQ
  - Solid elimination · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Standard Playfair**
  - Scope: Structural argument
  - Outcome: Ruled out by 26-letter ciphertext, KK doublet, odd length 97, and K->K
  - Solid elimination · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Hill cipher**
  - Scope: A-Z and KRYPTOS alphabets; 2x2 and 3x3 at every block phase; 4x4 at phases 1-2
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Solid elimination · gereon/kryptos, 2026-09 · [source](https://github.com/gereon/kryptos)
- **Hill cipher**
  - Scope: 2x2, 3x3, 4x4; A-Z and KRYPTOS indexing
  - Outcome: Ruled out except 4x4 at two block alignments the cribs cannot test; under guessed cribs OF or IS all four 4x4 alignments eliminated
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Trifid**
  - Scope: Any 3x3x3 cube, periods 2-24, every group offset
  - Outcome: 299/299 cases unsatisfiable
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Bifid, Playfair, two-square, four-square, ADFGX (5x5 squares)**
  - Scope: Structural argument
  - Outcome: Ruled out outright: K4 uses all 26 letters and a 5x5 square outputs 25
  - Solid elimination · keithadler/kryptos, 2026-09-29 · [source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/keithadler/kryptos/main/SEARCH.md)
- **Hill cipher over keyed alphabets**
  - Scope: KOMITE..., SANBOR..., JIMSANBOR..., CIPHER... and KRYPTOS alphabets
  - Outcome: No further English
  - Partial or weak test · Christian Schridde (NumberWorld), 2017 · [source](https://www.numberworld.blog/2017/03/kryptos-cipher-part-2.html)
- **Hill cipher (Bauer, Link and Molle 2016 conjecture)**
  - Scope: Argument only
  - Outcome: Argued against because 97 is prime
  - Partial or weak test · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **5x5 Polybius methods such as conjugated matrix bifid (suggested by multiple-of-5 distance patterns)**
  - Scope: Exploratory, scope not stated
  - Outcome: Nothing found
  - Partial or weak test · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Hill / affine Hill, and periodic key then Hill**
  - Scope: Hill and affine Hill 2x2 to 5x5; periodic key then Hill 2x2 or 3x3
  - Outcome: 0 hits / inconsistent; Hill >=6x6 remains open
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **6x6 Bifid**
  - Scope: Periods 2-97
  - Outcome: Infeasible
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **Hill cipher**
  - Scope: 2x2, 3x3 and 4x4 at every alignment, with and without the '?'
  - Outcome: Dead; all crib-forced 4x4 keys are non-invertible
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Bifid/Trifid with free squares and cubes**
  - Scope: Free squares/cubes; also Sovi11 and akaalias
  - Outcome: Mostly ruled out; a few hundred configurations remain feasible but give no English
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos, Sovi11, akaalias (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Trifid with dictionary-keyed cubes**
  - Scope: 17B hypotheses
  - Outcome: Catalog negative
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Fractionated Morse**
  - Scope: Fixed maps; also markmnl (https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
  - Outcome: Ruled out for fixed maps
  - Partial or weak test · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4, markmnl/kryptos (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Slidefair and Portax digraphic ciphers**
  - Scope: Tested forms
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **K4 is a Hill/matrix cipher (extra L in the tableau, HILL in rightmost column)**
  - Scope: Academic conjecture
  - Outcome: Not confirmed; later work refutes 2x2/3x3 (and keithadler 4x4 at testable alignments)
  - Disputed · Bauer, Link and Molle (Cryptologia), 2016 · [source](https://dx.doi.org/10.1080/01611194.2016.1141556)

## machine

- **Enigma-style or other no-self-encipherment (fixed-point-free) scheme as the final layer**
  - Scope: Logical argument from the CLOCK crib: ciphertext K at position 74 decrypts to plaintext K
  - Outcome: Excluded at least at position 74: a letter can encrypt to itself in K4
  - Solid elimination · Wikipedia (observation from 2014 crib), 2014 · [source](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryptos)
- **Gromark with a standard A-Z plaintext alphabet**
  - Scope: Crib constraint argument
  - Outcome: Ruled out: plain C and R both map to cipher P but are more than 10 places apart
  - Solid elimination · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Gromark key repeating at period 21 or 42 (and base < 3)**
  - Scope: Crib constraint argument
  - Outcome: Plain T->V (pos 24) and L->V (pos 66, 0-indexed) exclude periods 21 and 42 (63, 84 remain); base must be >=3
  - Solid elimination · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Wheatstone cryptograph with any outer ring of 27-38 cells and any 26-letter inner ring**
  - Scope: Proof: plain S,T give cipher S,S (letters 32-33) and plain S..T give cipher R,R (letters 23, 28), so n*m must be a multiple of 26 for ring size n and 1-5 turns m, which fails for n=27..38. Cross-check: 220,319 dictionary outer rings x reversed x 27-31 cells x 26 blank positions x spaces or not (114.6M settings)
  - Outcome: Dead: proof leaves no case; 0 dictionary rings consistent (3 shuffled K4s: 0); planted 3/3
  - Solid elimination · this repo (campaign 11), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_11.md
- **M-138-A strip cipher**
  - Scope: General proof about cyclic strips
  - Outcome: Ruled out by proof
  - Solid elimination · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Running key from a Wichmann-Hill PRNG**
  - Scope: Seeds 0-50 for each of s1, s2, s3 (full space ~30,000^3); Vigenere with various alphabets
  - Outcome: No hit; tiny fraction of the key space
  - Partial or weak test · Christian Schridde (NumberWorld), 2017 · [source](https://www.numberworld.blog/2017/03/kryptos-cipher-part-2.html)
- **Gromark base 10, 5-digit primer, with variable plain and cipher alphabets**
  - Scope: All 99,999 non-zero primers; crib inequalities + Groebner basis reduce to 39 primers; alphabets fitted by simulated annealing with hexagram scoring
  - Outcome: 39 primers survive; many near-English plaintexts, none convincing; primer reduction exact, alphabet search heuristic
  - Partial or weak test · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Gromark with other bases, primer lengths and key-expansion rules**
  - Scope: Base-10 4-digit primers (only 3301, 6740, 9903 possible); base-8 5-digit (4 remain); rules using first primer digit (Beker & Piper)
  - Outcome: None convincing
  - Partial or weak test · Richard Bean, 2021 · [source](https://ecp.ep.liu.se/index.php/histocrypt/article/download/153/109)
- **Keystream from a linear-feedback shift register (LFSR)**
  - Scope: Berlekamp-Massey linear complexity of K4 vs null texts
  - Outcome: K4 linear complexity 26-35 vs null 25-33: no LFSR of degree about 25 or less; 0 hits
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **Hebern rotor, Alberti stepping, Hagelin M-209/C-38**
  - Scope: Hebern N = 1-48
  - Outcome: All 0
  - Partial or weak test · zeyeteam-debug (Thanos Zekios with Claude), 2026 · [source](https://github.com/zeyeteam-debug/kryptos-k4-eliminations)
- **VIC-style chain-addition key**
  - Scope: 221,577 primers
  - Outcome: ~6,000 chance survivors, none explaining more than 2 of the 11 width-21 repeats
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Gromark (reproducing Bean 2021)**
  - Scope: Bases 3-26, primers of 2-8 digits, every two-lag recurrence (2.78B primers)
  - Outcome: 804,684 chance survivors, none explains the width-21 repeat pattern
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **M-94 wheel cipher**
  - Scope: All 25 published disks, all orders and offsets
  - Outcome: Dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10-17 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Chaocipher**
  - Scope: 1.02e10 rows
  - Outcome: Best 11/24, matched by shuffled text; full dictionary-by-dictionary run untested
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10-17 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Wheatstone cryptograph**
  - Scope: 21.9M outer rings
  - Outcome: Dead
  - Partial or weak test · Barneyjm/kryptos-k4, 2026-10-17 · [source](https://github.com/Barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Paper rotor with hidden wiring between two alphabets, turned by a key word (C = R[P+s] - s)**
  - Scope: wire-collision test, no wiring guessed; 676 constant speed pairs, letter-fed turning, 264k words x word/sliding/+1-per-letter x 2 numberings, every key of length 1-6; A-Z/KRYPTOS faces; 110.8M settings
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: 0 settings give a real wiring (3 shuffled K4s: 0); planted 3/3. About 1 true setting in 20 has too few wire collisions to be judged; keys longer than 6 only if dictionary words
  - Partial or weak test · this repo (campaign 11), 2026-11-16 · campaign/SUMMARY_11.md
- **Gromark-style chain addition with 3-term recurrences**
  - Scope: 2.27B three-term chains (beyond the 2-lag 2.78B-primer run already recorded)
  - Outcome: No signal
  - Partial or weak test · barneyjm/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/barneyjm/kryptos-k4)
- **Gromark / chain-addition key**
  - Scope: Bounded Gromark study; reproduces Bean-style survivors
  - Outcome: No signal: survivors can be completed several ways, so passing the cribs means nothing
  - Partial or weak test · reggiechan74/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **Binary LFSR output converted to letters**
  - Scope: Order <=30, several bit conventions. No direct URL in TRIED.md for this tester; cited via the reggiechan74 external prior-art register that TRIED.md drew on.
  - Outcome: Reported negative within bounds
  - Partial or weak test · ActiveTK (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/reggiechan74/kryptos-k4)
- **M-94 / Jefferson wheel cipher**
  - Scope: 25 disks, all orders, offsets; also akaalias
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4, akaalias (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
- **Hagelin M-209 / C-38**
  - Scope: Restricted versions only; also Scott (https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
  - Outcome: Restricted versions negative; full machine open
  - Partial or weak test · jcolinpatrick/kryptos, scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/jcolinpatrick/kryptos)
- **Chaocipher**
  - Scope: Up to 10 billion alphabet pairs; also akaalias
  - Outcome: Catalog negative; fully free wheels open
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos, akaalias (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Wheatstone cryptograph**
  - Scope: Tested forms; also akaalias
  - Outcome: Ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos, akaalias (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Constant-speed rotors, Alberti disks, historic rotor systems**
  - Scope: Tested forms; also Scott (https://github.com/scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4)
  - Outcome: Ruled out in tested forms
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos, scott-dunphy/kryptos-k4 (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)
- **Trigger-letter disks and plaintext-triggered resets**
  - Scope: Tested forms
  - Outcome: Catalog negative / ruled out
  - Partial or weak test · markmnl/kryptos (via TRIED.md compilation), by 2026-11 · [source](https://github.com/markmnl/kryptos)

- **K2 ending 'ID BY ROWS' is an instruction for K4**

  - Solid elimination · Jim Sanborn (correction), 2006-04-20 · [source](http://www.wired.com/news/technology/0,70701-0.html)
- **One wrong crib letter, or 1-3 letters inserted/dropped, with repeating, progressive and running keys**
  - Scope: Repeating, progressive and running-key models
  - Outcome: Ruled out: 580 survivors vs 516 expected, none above 2 constraints

## Audit holes closed (attacks 90, 59, 88, 60C, 61, 69B, 57)

- **K4 is one of the simple remembered-key ciphers sitting in a region that idea 105 showed the old attacks never really tested: row-keys-row autokey with cipher feed 30-33 back plus a word of 2-4, autokey + repeating key with cipher feed 34-60 or plain feed 7-8, a progressive key running over word spaces, Slidefair with same-column pairs, a key scrambled by a clockwise K3 turn, rows of 21 with a different tableau per row keyed by letters, or an English running key set under a letter other than the strip's first.**
  - Scope: Fixed copies of the test functions (kryptos/idea118_holes.py), run only on the missed settings: 90 440.2M settings; 59 56,400; 88 progressive 216.2G exact tests (220,318 alphabets x 10 streams x 3,926 position sets x 25 steps, each exact); 60C 112; 61 5.0M; 69B join over 2.2M x 2.2M streams x 26 letters; 57 132.2M readings scored as English. 7+ checks (59 and 61 also at their own 6).
  - Outcome: Dead: K4 0 passes in every hole (chance predicts 0.0002 / 0.00004 / 7e-7 / 7e-14 / 0.0008 / 0.71 for 90 / 59 / 88 / 60C / 61 / 69B); 57 best -4.841 vs shuffled -4.908 to -4.989, not English (controls: 3 shuffled K4s on thirds 0 passes, 10 shuffles for 59/60C/61 0 passes; planted: 282/312 hole plants found, 30 unjudgeable, 0 missed; 8/8 read back; clue one letter late 0/52).
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 118), 2026-11-18 · campaign/swarm/118.md

## Autokey a worksheet row back

- **Each row of the K4 worksheet is keyed by the row above: the key letter is the plaintext or ciphertext letter 7, 8 or about 42 places earlier, so the first clue is the key of the second.**
  - Scope: Exact test on the clue letters, 7+ checks. Plain feed lags 7, 8 and 34-48 (second clue moved -3..3, 8+ pairs); cipher feed lags 1-66 (clue moved -3..3 for 34-48); constant or repeating word of 2-4 on top; 666,993 alphabets (A-Z, KRYPTOS, every dictionary keyword alphabet and its two transposition-mixed forms) x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort x fed letter numbered in M/A-Z/KRYPTOS x added or subtracted; 31.6 billion settings.
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: 1 of 31.6 billion settings passes, at the 7-check floor (cipher feed 61 back, word of 4), and its 36 forced letters are noise (-8.28 against -5.1 needed); about one such pass is expected by chance (shuffled K4s: 0 on a third of the alphabets each; planted 5/5). Not decided: plain feed at lags 9-33 (too few or no clue pairs).
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 90), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/90.md

## Autokey that looks ahead (message enciphered back to front)

- **K4 was enciphered from its last letter backwards with an autokey, so each key letter is the plaintext or ciphertext letter d places further on (at d = 42 BERLINCLOCK keys EASTNORTHEAST).**
  - Scope: Exact test on the clue letters, 7+ checks. Plain feed d = 1-8, and d = 34-48 with the second clue moved -3..3 (8+ pairs); cipher feed d = 1-68; constant or repeating word of 2-4 on top; 666,993 alphabets (A-Z, KRYPTOS, every dictionary keyword alphabet and its two transposition-mixed forms) x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort x fed letter numbered in M/A-Z/KRYPTOS x added or subtracted: 18.49 billion settings. Also on A-Z/KRYPTOS with any multiplier 1-25 on the fed letter and a word of 1-13 (333,200 settings), and with one hidden alphabet (both sides, or one side against A-Z/KRYPTOS) at d = 1-8, distinct letter places required (320 models).
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: 0 settings pass on dictionary tableaux, 0 on A-Z/KRYPTOS with multipliers (best 7 of 24 pairs on one constant), 0 hidden-alphabet models with 3+ checks; 48 hidden-alphabet models undecidable (controls: 3 shuffled K4s on all alphabets 0 each, near miss 6-7 of 24; 20 shuffled K4s on the hidden test 0-5 survivors; planted 15/15). Not decided: plain feed at d = 9-33.
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 96), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/96.md

## Cipher letter as a distance through the sculpture's text (skip-count / book-style reading)

- **Each K4 letter is a count (its place in A-Z or KRYPTOS order) to step on through a text already on the sculpture, and the letter landed on is the plaintext; either from a window that moves a fixed amount per letter or as a running walk.**
  - Scope: P_i = T[s + a*i +- d(C_i)] and P_i = T[q_i], q_i = q_(i-1) + a +- d(C_i); 39 texts (K1-K3 plaintexts and ciphertext, cipher panel, tableau panel, Morse, panels joined, panels by columns/back-and-forth/diagonals) x forwards/backwards x every start x a = 0..31 x A-Z/KRYPTOS numbering x both count directions, ring and line; 16,060,464 settings.
  - Outcome: Dead: best 11/24 clue letters where 24 are needed, readings gibberish (controls: 20 shuffled K4s give 9-11; planted 7/7 found at 24/24 and read back). Also: ordinary English can almost never be encoded this way on these texts (0 of 200 messages by either rule on the K1-K3 plaintext or the cipher panel; a walk dies after about 18 letters), so the method is not workable on prose however it is set.
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 85), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/85.md

## Cipher letter as a word count through the solved K1-K3 text (prisoner letter code / book-style reading)

- **Each K4 letter is a number of words to count through the K1-K3 plaintext (or the Morse text), and one fixed letter of the word reached (first, second, third, fourth or last) is the plaintext; as a running walk, as a window that moves a fixed number of words per letter, or with a neighbouring K4 letter giving the letter number.**
  - Scope: P_i = letter k of word s + a*i +- d(C_i) (window) or of word s + a*i +- (d(C_0)+..+d(C_i)) (walk); 56 word lists (K1, K2, K3, joins in order, Morse; carved/corrected spelling, X kept/dropped, both K2 endings); k = 1-4 or last; d in A-Z or KRYPTOS order, both directions; a = -30..30; every start; ring and line: 10,891,184 settings. Overlapping pairs (word from C_i, letter number from C_(i+-1), 16 letter numberings): 53,484,800 settings. Halved pairs: 876,800 readings searched for the clue words.
  - Outcome: Dead: best 10/24 clue letters where 24 are needed (walk 9, window 10, pairs 10), readings gibberish at -6.5 on the big model (controls: 20 shuffled K4s give 9-11 and 10-11; planted 6/6 found at 24/24 and read back). Also: ordinary English cannot be encoded by counting words through K1-K3 with a fixed letter position (0 of 200 messages by walk or window, a walk dies after 3-6 letters), so the method is unworkable on this text however it is set.
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 106), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/106.md

## Few-symbol keys (digits in a base, clock numerals, Roman numerals) on keyword tableaux

- **K4's key is a list of numbers written with few symbols (digits in base 2-16, clock hours 1-12 or Roman numeral letters), so on the right keyword tableau the 24 key values forced by the clues fall in a small window of consecutive values or on 7 or fewer values, whatever rule the numbers follow.**
  - Scope: 666,993 alphabets (A-Z, KRYPTOS, dictionary keyword alphabets and their two transposition-mixed forms) x 5 layouts x 4 views (C-P, C+P, P-C, -(C+P)) = 13,339,856 settings; smallest circular window of the 24 key values, number of distinct values, cover by windows of 5-16 and by any 2-7 values, digits 0-9 and smallest base for both signs, Roman letters I V X L C D M with the key letter read in A-Z, KRYPTOS or the tableau alphabet; each clue block alone
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: K4's smallest window is 12 (3 streams, none at 11 or less), fewest distinct values 8, most Roman letters 19 of 24, most digits 22 of 24 (controls: 50 shuffled K4s give smallest windows 10-14 with 17 at 12 or less, distinct 7-9, Roman 18-21, digits 19-23; planted 4/4 flagged with the exact key, a planted digit key lights up 38 neighbouring streams at 12 or less where K4 has 3). Windows of 10 or less and 7 or fewer values are cleanly absent; a window of exactly 12 occurs by chance in a third of shuffles, so bases 12-16 are ruled out only by the missing cluster and the patternless key values.
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 97), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/97.md

## Formula key with a hidden tableau alphabet

- **K4's key follows a short formula in the position or a grid (progressive, quadratic, row and column of a grid, a number sequence) and the tableau alphabet is an unknown mixed alphabet rather than A-Z or KRYPTOS.**
  - Scope: k = b1*f1 + b2*f2 + b3*f3 + c mod 26, up to 3 of 134 ingredients (position powers, triangular, primes, Fibonacci, powers of 2 and 3, pi and e digits, column/row/row*column for widths 2-40, carved layout), b = 1..25, any constant; one hidden alphabet on both sides in Vigenere and Beaufort form (4 exact loop tests, then 21 letters on different positions) and a hidden plain or cipher alphabet against A-Z or KRYPTOS (10-11 exact tests); 170.9 billion settings

## first-part-only small-shift key on a hidden plain alphabet

- **Letters 1..E of K4 (E before BERLINCLOCK) were enciphered with a key of small numbers (0-4 or 0-5) on a hidden mixed plain alphabet against A-Z cipher letters, which is why EASTNORTHEAST alone fits a window of 5.**
  - Scope: ptX/AZ layout, window 5 and 6. Free key: beam width 2000 on the big quadgram model over letters 1-21 and 35-E, E=49-63, alphabet kept exactly as letter domains plus a matching. Periodic key, period 2-13 (w=5) / 2-11 (w=6): every setting the clue block allows, solved by hill climb for E=49 and 63. 10 shuffled K4s with the same w=5 first block (plus 100 more for the free-key score).
  - Outcome: Dead in scope: periods 2-6 impossible at w=5 and 6, period 9 impossible; best periodic reading -4.61 (w=5) / -4.81 (w=6), not English and inside the matched controls (K4 beats 9/10 and 4/6); free-key beam score inside 100 matched controls (45/100 at w=5, 88/100 at w=6), readings word salad (planted periodic 3/3 recovered, 2 at rank 1 and 1 at rank 2; planted free-key texts beat their own shuffles 56/60 but are not read back).
  - Partial or weak test · this repo (swarm, idea 133), 2026-11-18 · campaign/swarm/133.md

## flawed tableau

- **K4 was enciphered on a tableau with one extra or missing letter (in the header, the row labels, the strip, or one row such as the carved row N with its extra L), so every lookup past the flaw is one cell off, with an ordinary repeating, sliding, progressive or autokey key.**
  - Scope: 220,318 alphabets (A-Z, KRYPTOS, plain dictionary keyword alphabets) x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort; header, row and strip flaws at every cell t=1..25, d=+1/-1, both candidate key values tried where the flaw makes two possible; single-row flaw at every row and column threshold on A-Z/KRYPTOS (Vigenere); repeating 1-17 (not row flaws), sliding 1-15, progressive 1-12, autokey lags 1-6; 7+ exact checks on the 24 clue letters.
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 of 169,309,207 corrected streams pass with 7+ checks, chance 0.032 (controls 0/0/0 of 57M-101M streams each; planted 4/4 with the flaw cell named)
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 114), 2026-11-18 · campaign/swarm/114.md

## free per-letter choice of system (Vig/Beau/variant or two tableaux)

- **K4 uses one repeating (or sliding) key, and at each letter the encoder freely chose between Vigenere, Beaufort and variant Beaufort on one keyword tableau, or between two tableaux, so the reader tries each and keeps what reads.**
  - Scope: A-Z, KRYPTOS and 220,316 dictionary keyword alphabets; 5 layouts (M/M, M/AZ, AZ/M, M/KR, KR/M); choice sets {V,B}, {V,var}, {B,var}, {V,B,var}; tableau choice M|AZ and M|KR in one form or Vig/Beau on both; repeating key L 1-17, sliding key L 1-17 (form choice only); bar = strength 7, where strength = -log26 of the expected chance solutions
  - Outcome: Dead for 2-way choice with repeating key length up to 11 (sliding up to 10), 3-way up to 9 (sliding 8), 4-way up to 7 (longer where the alphabet allows: 2-way up to 14): 0 of 167.6M settings at strength 7+ and 0 at 5+ (controls: 3 shuffled K4s on a third of the alphabets each, 0 and 0; planted 4/4 found and read back 90-98%). Longer keys up to 17 tested but too weak to decide.
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 87), 2026-11-18 · campaign/swarm/87.md

## gap slack: formula keys and Quagmire IV

- **A short linear key formula, or a three-keyword Quagmire IV, fits the clues once the second clue is moved by 1-3 places because letters were lost or added between the clues.**
  - Scope: Second clue at 63+g..73+g for g = -3,-2,-1,+1,+2,+3. Formulas C = a*P + up to 3 of 186 ingredients + c mod 26 (A-Z/KRYPTOS, 8+ checks), 25.7 million formulas. Quagmire IV hash join over 666,993 x 666,993 dictionary keyword alphabets (plain and two transposition mixes), Vigenere/Beaufort, key lengths 1-20, 1.07e14 settings
  - Outcome: Dead: formulas 0 fits on K4 (2 shuffled K4s per shift: 0); join 13,091 chance-level matches on K4 at key lengths 15-20, best partial decrypt -5.95, 0 dictionary-word keys (shuffled: 12,254 and 452 matches, best -5.97 and -5.96, 0 word keys); planted 6/6, each found only at its true shift and not with the carved gap
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 79), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/79.md

## guessed crib

  - Scope: repeating keys 1-60, sliding keys 1-40, two stacked keys every pair p<q<=26 (exact linear solve), autokey with one hidden alphabet at lags 1-8 (real alphabet required); Vigenere/Beaufort, A-Z/KRYPTOS; 14,720 settings

## homophone cycling / counters

- **K4 is C = Tab(P, W[n mod L]) with n the number of earlier occurrences of the cipher letter, so the key word steps on with each repeat of the cipher letter.**
  - Scope: Counts from K4's start and from every point of K1K2K3's ciphertext (769 starts), L = 1..26, one key word; tableau-free proof on all settings, then 666,993 keyword alphabets x 5 layouts x Vig/Beau/variant, joined with 264k words and K1-K3 phrase windows (any rotation), and W free.
  - Outcome: Dead: impossible for every tableau when counting from K4's start (E->F and E->G at 22 and 31, both with n = 1) or from K1's start; at the other starts 0 of 6.67M key streams are consistent (chance 0.022), 0 word fits (controls: 3 shuffled K4s on thirds, consistent 2, 0, 0, word fits 0, 0, 0, best W-free reading -7.02; planted 4/4 named and read back exactly). Also: English cannot be enciphered this way (0 of 1,000 messages get past 30 letters).
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 140), 2026-11-18 · campaign/swarm/140.md

- **Interrupted key: a repeating key that restarts at/after a trigger plaintext or ciphertext letter, or at each word**
  - Scope: key length 1-26; every trigger letter present in the clues; unknown key phase per clue block; Vigenere/Beaufort/variant; A-Z/KRYPTOS each side + 110 theme-word keyword alphabets (Quagmire III style); 247,104 rules, exact

## interrupted key: one place lost inside a clue word

- **K4 is a periodic-key cipher on a keyword tableau in which Sanborn lost his place in the key once inside EASTNORTHEAST or BERLINCLOCK (one key letter skipped or used twice), so every periodic test on that block fails.**
  - Scope: Slip before any letter of a clue block, +1 or -1 key place. Repeating key, sliding key with the slip in the key word, sliding key with the slip in the running key. A: one block alone with a slip, 7+ checks (L 2-6 / 2-4). B: one slip in either block + any key offset between the blocks, L 2-12. C: one slip in each block + any offset, L 2-12. A-Z, KRYPTOS and 666,991 dictionary keyword alphabets incl. transposition-mixed x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort = 6,669,928 key streams, 799,884,445,472 exact settings
  - Outcome: Dead: 0 settings with 7+ checks on K4 (chance expects 0.11; 3 shuffled K4s on a third of the alphabets and 6 on all of them: 0 each); near misses with 5-6 checks: K4 199 against 48-211 on six full-size shuffled K4s (chance expects 82), best decryption -7.34 per quadgram (shuffled -7.09 to -7.35; English above -5.1); planted slips found with the slip point named 4/4, three read back 97/97
  - Solid elimination · this repo (swarm, idea 102), 2026-11-17 · campaign/swarm/102.md

A-Z, KRYPTOS and 220,316 dictionary keyword alphabets x 5 layouts x Vigenere/Beaufort = 2,203,178 key streams, 343,695,768 settings
